The Lee-Huang-Yang energy for a dilute gas of hard spheres: an upper bound
Giulia Basti, Morris Brooks, Serena Cenatiempo, Alessandro Olgiati, Benjamin Schlein

Abstract
We consider a quantum gas consisting of N N hard spheres with radius 𝔞 > 0 \mathfrak{a}>0 , obeying bosonic statistics and moving in the box Λ = [ 0 ; L ] 3 \Lambda=[0;L]^{3} with periodic boundary conditions. We are interested in the ground state energy per unit volume in the thermodynamic limit, with N , L → ∞ N,L\to\infty at fixed density ρ = N / L 3 \rho=N/L^{3} . We derive an upper bound for the ground state energy density, matching the famous Lee-Huang-Yang formula, up to lower order terms, in the dilute limit ρ ​ 𝔞 3 ≪ 1 \rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\ll 1 .



1
Introduction

Since the early days of quantum mechanics, the study of dilute Bose gases has played a central role in condensed matter physics. Based on the work of Bogoliubov
[
9
]
, Lee-Huang-Yang
[
29
]
predicted in 1957 that the ground state energy per unit volume of a Bose gas at density
\rho>0
is given by

e(\rho)=4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}+\dots\Big]

(1.1)

up to smaller order corrections, in the dilute limit
\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\to 0
. Here
\mathfrak{a}>0
is the scattering length of the interaction potential.

At the level of rigorous mathematics, the validity of the leading order term in the expansion (
1.1
) has been established by Dyson
[
17
]
, as an upper bound, and by Lieb-Yngvason
[
31
]
, as a lower bound, for general repulsive interactions. At positive temperature, leading order estimates for the free energy of a dilute gas have been derived in
[
37
]
(lower bound) and
[
40
,
2
]
(upper bounds).

In the last years, the focus has shifted towards the rigorous justification of the second order contribution to the ground state energy density, known as the Lee-Huang-Yang term. For particles interacting through weak, mean-field type, potentials, second order expansions for the ground state energy have been established in
[
23
,
37
,
30
,
15
,
34
]
. In the Gross-Pitaevskii regime, where
N
particles move in a volume of order one and interact through a potential with scattering length of the order
1/N
, the ground state energy has been determined to the Lee-Huang-Yang order in
[
7
,
8
,
10
,
32
,
11
,
26
,
12
]
. In this case, even the third order correction to the ground state energy, predicted in 1959 by Wu
[
38
]
and by Hugenholtz-Pines
[
27
]
, has been recently established; see
[
14
]
.

In the thermodynamic limit, at low but fixed density
\rho>0
, a lower bound matching (
1.1
) up to the Lee-Huang-Yang order has been first shown in
[
20
]
, for particles interacting through a repulsive, integrable potential, and then in
[
21
]
, for more general interactions, including the hard-sphere potential. Very recently, these results have been also extended to low but positive temperatures; see
[
24
,
19
]
.

In the same thermodynamic setting, an upper bound capturing the Lee-Huang-Yang term has been first proven in
[
39
]
, assuming regular potential. A simpler second order upper bound for the ground state energy density has been later derived in
[
5
]
and extended to positive temperatures in
[
25
]
. Very recently, a third order upper bound, capturing the Lee-Huang-Yang term and also Wu’s contribution, has been obtained in
[
13
]
.

Compared with
[
39
]
, the upper bounds in
[
5
,
25
,
13
]
cover a larger class of interactions, but they still require integrability and thus they exclude the physically interesting case of a hard-sphere potential. For hard spheres, the best available upper bound resolves the ground state energy per unit volume up to the Lee-Huang-Yang order, but it does not achieve the correct constant; see
[
3
]
.

In this paper, we fill the gap in the literature, proving an upper bound for the ground state energy density of hard spheres, matching (
1.1
), up to lower order corrections. Together with the lower bound from
[
21
]
, this shows the validity of the Lee-Huang-Yang formula (
1.1
) for the hard-sphere gas in the dilute limit.

We consider a system of
N
hard spheres, moving in the torus
\Lambda\simeq[-L/2;L/2]^{3}
. The ground state energy of the gas is given by

E_{N}^{\text{hs}}=\inf\frac{1}{\|\psi_{N}\|^{2}}\big\langle\psi_{N},\sum_{j=1}^{N}-\Delta_{x_{j}}\Psi_{N}\big\rangle

where the infimum is taken over all
\psi_{N}\in L^{2}_{s}(\Lambda^{N})
, the subspace of
L^{2}(\Lambda^{N})
consisting of functions that are symmetric with respect to permutations, satisfying the hard-core condition

\psi_{N}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})=0,\quad\text{if there are $i,j\in\{1,\dots,N\}$ with $|x_{i}-x_{j}|\leq\mathfrak{a}$\,.}

(1.2)

We are interested in the ground state per unit volume at density
\rho>0
, defined by the limit

e(\rho)=\lim_{\begin{subarray}{c}N,L\to\infty\\
N/L^{3}=\rho\end{subarray}}\frac{E_{N}^{\text{hs}}}{L^{3}}\,,

(1.3)

which is well-known to exist, see
[
35
, Thm 3.5.11]
.

Theorem 1.1
.

For
\rho>0
, let
e(\rho)
be defined as in (
1.3
). Then there exists
C>0
and a sufficiently small
\delta>0
such that

e(\rho)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}+C(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2+\delta}\Big]

(1.4)

for all
\rho>0
small enough.

Remark.
In contrast to
[
39
,
5
,
25
,
13
]
, where the trial state on
\Lambda
was constructed by pasting together a large number of identical states defined on smaller boxes, with size independent of
L
, here we work directly in the thermodynamic box.

The proof of Theorem
1.1
is based on the construction of an appropriate trial state and on the computation of its energy. As first proposed in
[
6
,
16
,
28
]
, to approximate the ground state of a dilute gas it is natural to consider so-called Jastrow factors having the form

\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})=\prod_{i<j}^{N}f_{\ell}(x_{i}-x_{j})\,

(1.5)

with
f_{\ell}
describing two-particle correlations produced by the interaction, up to a length scale
\ell\gg\mathfrak{a}
. For hard spheres, an obvious choice is given by

f_{\ell}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1-\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|}\chi_{\ell}(x)&\text{if $|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}$},\\
0,&\text{if $|x|\leq\mathfrak{a}$}\end{array}\right.

(1.6)

with a cutoff
\chi_{\ell}(x)=\chi(x/\ell)
, where
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{0}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with
\chi(x)=1
, if
|x|\leq 2
, and
\chi(x)=0
, for
|x|\geq 4
.
For (
1.5
), we can compute

\nabla_{x_{j}}\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})=\sum_{i\not=j}\frac{\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})}{f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})}\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})

which leads us to the kinetic energy

\begin{split}\langle\Psi_{\text{J}},&\sum_{j=1}^{N}-\Delta_{x_{j}}\Psi_{\text{J}}\rangle\\
=\;&2\sum_{i<j}^{N}\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,\frac{|\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})|^{2}}{f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{j}-x_{i})}|\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})|^{2}\\
&+\sum_{i,j,m}^{N}\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,\frac{\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})}{f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})}\cdot\frac{\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{m})}{f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{m})}|\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots.,x_{N})|^{2}\end{split}

(1.7)

where the sum on the last line runs over
1\leq i,j,m\leq N
all different. With the choice (
1.6
), we find (see Lemma
2.1
below)

\|\nabla f_{\ell}\|^{2}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}\Big)\,.

(1.8)

Recalling that
0\leq f_{\ell}(x)\leq 1
, we obtain

\begin{split}2\sum_{i<j}\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,&\frac{|\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{j}-x_{i})|^{2}}{f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{j}-x_{i})}|\Psi_{\text{J}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{N})|^{2}\\
\leq\;&N^{2}\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,|\nabla f_{\ell}(x_{1}-x_{2})|^{2}\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq N}f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{i}-x_{j})\\
\leq\;&4\pi\mathfrak{a}N^{2}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}\Big)\int dx_{2}\dots dx_{N}\,\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq N}f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{i}-x_{j})\end{split}

and, since
0\leq 1-f_{\ell}^{2}(x)\leq C\mathfrak{a}/|x|
,

\begin{split}\|\Psi_{\text{J}}\|^{2}&=\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,\prod_{i<j}^{N}f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{i}-x_{j})\\
&\geq\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{N}\,\Big(1-\sum_{m=2}^{N}(1-f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{1}-x_{m}))\Big)\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq N}f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{i}-x_{j})\\
&\geq\Big(L^{3}-CN\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2}\Big)\int dx_{2}\dots dx_{N}\,\prod_{2\leq i<j\leq N}f_{\ell}^{2}(x_{i}-x_{j})\,.\end{split}

(1.9)

Proceeding similarly to bound the second term on the r.h.s. of (
1.7
) and noticing that
L^{3}-CN\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2}=L^{3}(1-C\rho\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2})
, we arrive at

\liminf_{\begin{subarray}{c}N,L\to\infty:\\
N/L^{3}=\rho\end{subarray}}\;\frac{1}{L^{3}}\frac{\langle\Psi_{\text{J}},\sum_{j=1}^{N}-\Delta_{x_{j}}\Psi_{\text{J}}\rangle}{\|\Psi_{\text{J}}\|^{2}}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}+C\rho\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2}\Big)\,.

(1.10)

With the optimal choice
\ell=\rho^{-1/3}
, we derive the bound

e(\rho)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}(1+C(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/3})

(1.11)

first proven by Dyson in
[
17
]
(in his work, Dyson used a non-symmetric version of (
1.5
)). Notice that (
1.10
) crucially relies on cancellations between huge but identical factors appearing both in the expectation of the energy (in the numerator) and in the norm of
\Psi_{\text{J}}
(in the denominator) on the l.h.s. of the inequality.

This simple computation captures the correct leading order contribution to the ground state energy per unit volume, but it misses the Lee-Huang-Yang corrections. Through a systematic cluster-type expansion of (
1.5
) to sufficiently high order, the cutoff in (
1.6
) can be increased to
\ell=c(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
, with
c>0
small enough, leading to an upper bound resolving the ground state energy per unit volume up to corrections of the Lee-Huang-Yang order; see
[
3
]
. In order to capture the correct Lee-Huang-Yang term with the trial state (
1.5
), though, one would need to include the correct correlation structure in
f_{\ell}
up to a length scale
\ell
large in comparison with the healing length
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
; this seems quite a challenge, since the convergence of the expansion used in
[
3
]
requires
\rho\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2}\ll 1
.

For “soft” interaction potentials, a different approach to derive upper bounds on the ground state energy has been proposed in
[
22
]
and, more recently, in
[
18
]
. It is based on the use of Bogoliubov transformations to model correlations, up to and beyond the healing length scale
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
. While quasi-free states generated by Bogoliubov maps cannot reach the desired precision
[
33
]
, they can resolve the Lee-Huang-Yang term after appropriate modifications by means of cubic transformations describing scattering processes involving a particle in the Bose-Einstein condensate and three of its orthogonal excitations; see

[
39
,
5
]
.

Unfortunately, it is problematic to extend this approach to the hard-sphere potential, because it is difficult to enforce the condition (
1.2
) on a Bogoliubov state. Still, taking inspiration from the soft-potential case, it makes sense to modify (
1.5
), keeping the Jastrow factor on short length scales
\mathfrak{a}\ll\ell\ll(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
(making sure in particular that the hard-core condition (
1.2
) is satisfied), but multiplying it with a Bogoliubov state, describing correlations up to (and just beyond) the healing length
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
. This attempt works in the Gross-Pitaevskii scaling
[
4
]
, where the effective density vanishes as
N\to\infty
. In the thermodynamic limit, on the other hand, it is not clear whether it can work, because the presence of the Bogoliubov state makes it difficult to identify cancellations between numerator and denominator that played an important role in (
1.10
).

In this paper, we follow a different approach, working in the grand canonical ensemble on the bosonic Fock space
\mathcal{F}=\bigoplus_{n\geq 0}L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes_{s}n}
. In this setting, we implement the Jastrow factor (
1.5
) through the operator
J:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
, defined by

(J\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\prod_{i<j}^{n}f_{\ell}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Phi^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})

for all
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
\Phi\in\mathcal{F}
. For any
x\in\Lambda
, the action of the annihilation operator
a_{x}
on
J
is given by

a_{x}J=J(x)Ja_{x}

(1.12)

where
J(x):\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
is defined through

(J(x)\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})\Phi^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})

for all
x\in\Lambda
(the identity (
1.12
) will be shown in Section
2
, where we will also introduce the formalism of second quantization, with precise definitions of annihilation and creation operators).

From the equivalence of ensembles in the thermodynamic limit, to prove the upper bound (
1.4
) for the ground state energy per unit volume, it is enough to construct a trial state
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
, with expected number of particles
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle\geq\rho L^{3}
and with kinetic energy per unit volume matching (
1.4
). As a first (too) simple attempt to reach this goal, we define the trial state

\widetilde{\Psi}=\frac{1}{Z}JW(\rho_{0})\Omega

(1.13)

where
Z=\|JW(\rho_{0})\Omega\|
is a normalization constant,
\Omega=\{1,0,0,\dots\}
is the vacuum vector in
\mathcal{F}
and

W(\rho_{0})=e^{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\int dx(a_{x}^{*}-a_{x})}

is a Weyl operator, generating the coherent state
W(\rho_{0})\Omega
, modeling a Bose-Einstein condensate with, in average,
N_{0}=\rho_{0}L^{3}
particles. Recalling that coherent states are eigenvectors of creation and annihilation operators, we find

a_{x}\widetilde{\Psi}=\frac{1}{Z}a_{x}JW(\rho_{0})\Omega=\frac{1}{Z}J(x)Ja_{x}W(\rho_{0})\Omega=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}J(x)\widetilde{\Psi}\,.

(1.14)

This allows us to compute the expectation of the kinetic energy, defined on
\mathcal{F}
as the second quantization of the Laplacian, in the state (
1.13
):

\begin{split}\langle\widetilde{\Psi},&\,d\Gamma(-\Delta)\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle\\
=\;&\int dx\,\langle\nabla_{x}a_{x}\widetilde{\Psi},\nabla_{x}a_{x}\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle=\rho_{0}\int dx\,\langle\nabla_{x}J(x)\widetilde{\Psi},\nabla_{x}J(x)\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle\\
=\;&\rho_{0}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\int dx\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{n}\,\frac{|\nabla f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})|^{2}}{f_{\ell}^{2}(x-x_{j})}\prod_{m=1}^{n}f_{\ell}^{2}(x-x_{m})|\widetilde{\Psi}^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})|^{2}\\
&+\rho_{0}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{i<j}^{n}\int dx\int dx_{1}\dots dx_{n}\,\frac{\nabla f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})}{f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})}\cdot\frac{\nabla f_{\ell}(x-x_{i})}{f_{\ell}(x-x_{i})}\prod_{m=1}^{n}f_{\ell}^{2}(x-x_{m})\\
&\hskip 256.0748pt\times|\widetilde{\Psi}^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})|^{2}\\
=:\,&\text{I}+\text{II}\,.\end{split}

Since
0\leq f_{\ell}(x)\leq 1
and recalling (
1.8
), the first term on the r.h.s. is bounded by

\begin{split}\text{I}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}\Big)\sum_{n}n\|\widetilde{\Psi}^{(n)}\|^{2}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}N_{0}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}\Big)\,.\end{split}

As for the second term, we apply (
1.14
) and observe that
J(x),J(y)\leq 1
(and that
a_{x}J(y)=f_{\ell}(x-y)J(y)a_{x}
, as shown below in (
2.9
)) to conclude that

\begin{split}\text{II}\leq\;&C\frac{\rho_{0}}{\ell}\sum_{n\geq 1}\sum_{i<j}^{n}\int_{|x_{i}-x_{j}|\leq C\ell}dx_{1}\dots dx_{n}\,|\widetilde{\Psi}^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})|^{2}\\
=\;&C\frac{\rho_{0}}{\ell}\int_{|x-y|\leq C\ell}dxdy\,\langle\widetilde{\Psi},a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}a_{y}a_{x}\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle\leq C\rho_{0}^{2}\ell^{2}N_{0}\,.\end{split}

It follows that

\frac{1}{L^{3}}\langle\widetilde{\Psi},d\Gamma(-\Delta)\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}^{2}\Big(1+C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell}+C\rho_{0}\mathfrak{a}\ell^{2}\Big)\,.

Choosing
\rho_{0}=\rho
(in fact, we need to choose
\rho_{0}=\rho(1+C\rho)
a bit larger than
\rho
, to make sure that
\langle\widetilde{\Psi},\mathcal{N}\widetilde{\Psi}\rangle=\rho L^{3}
; the difference in the energy is subleading, though) and
\ell=\rho^{-1/3}
, we obtain again Dyson’s bound (
1.11
).

This computation is similar to the one leading to (
1.10
) for the energy of the
N
-particle Jastrow factor (
1.5
). It has however one crucial difference: it does not require cancellations of huge normalization factors between numerator and denominator. Thanks to this observation, we will be able to modify the simple trial state (
1.13
), without worrying about messing up the normalization. Instead of (
1.13
), we will consider the trial state
\Psi=Z^{-1}JW(\rho_{0})T\Omega
, inserting the Bogoliubov transformation
T
, generating correlations at large scales, up to (and just beyond) the healing length
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
. Since the action of
T
on creation and annihilation operators is explicit, we will again be able to derive an identity, more complicated than (
1.14
) but still simple enough, for the action of
a_{x}
on
\Psi
. We will use this identity to estimate the number of particles and the kinetic energy of
\Psi
; this will lead us to (
1.4
).

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we introduce the formalism of second quantization, we define precisely our trial state
\Psi
and we state bounds on the expectation of number of particles and kinetic energy operators in
\Psi
. In Section
3
, we show that these bounds imply our main result, Theorem
1.1
. In Section
4
, we establish precise estimates on the distribution of the local number of particles and the local number of excitations in the state
\Psi
. These estimates immediately imply the desired bound on the global number of particles. Moreover, they give us the main tools to control error terms arising in the computation of the energy of
\Psi
, which is deferred to Section
5
.

2
The Trial State

We consider the bosonic Fock space

\mathcal{F}=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{+\infty}L^{2}(\Lambda)^{\otimes_{\mathrm{sym}}n}

constructed on the one-particle Hilbert space
L^{2}(\Lambda)
, where
\Lambda=[-L/2;L/2]^{3}
, with periodic boundary conditions.

On
\mathcal{F}
, we define the number of particle operator
\mathcal{N}=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}n
and the kinetic energy operator
\mathcal{K}=\bigoplus_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{j=1}^{n}-\Delta_{x_{j}}
.

Moreover, for
f\in L^{2}(\Lambda)
we introduce the creation and annihilation operators
a^{*}(f),a(f)
. For
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, their action is defined by

\begin{split}(a(f)\Psi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})&=\sqrt{n+1}\int_{\Lambda}dx\bar{f}(x)\Psi^{(n+1)}(x,x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\\
(a^{*}(f)\Psi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})&=\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=1}^{n}f(x_{j})\Psi^{(n-1)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{j-1},x_{j+1},\dots,x_{n})\,.\end{split}

They satisfy the canonical commutation relations

\big[a(f),a^{*}(g)\big]=\langle f,g\rangle,\qquad\big[a(f),a(g)\big]=\big[a^{*}(f),a^{*}(g)\big]=0

for every
f,g\in L^{2}(\Lambda)
. It is also convenient to define operator-valued distributions
a_{x},a_{x}^{*}
,
x\in\Lambda
, so that

a(f)=\int\bar{f}(x)a_{x}\,dx,\qquad a^{*}(f)=\int f(x)a_{x}^{*}\,dx

and

[a_{x},a_{y}^{*}]=\delta(x-y),\qquad[a_{x},a_{y}]=[a_{x}^{*},a_{y}^{*}]=0

for all
x,y\in\Lambda
. Explicitly, the action of
a_{x}
is described by

(a_{x}\Psi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\sqrt{n+1}\,\Psi^{(n+1)}(x,x_{1},\dots,x_{n})

(2.1)

for arbitrary
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. In terms of these operator-valued distributions, we can write

\mathcal{N}=\int dx\,a_{x}^{*}a_{x}\,,\qquad\mathcal{K}=\int dx\nabla_{x}a_{x}^{*}\nabla_{x}a_{x}\,.

(2.2)

In this section, we construct a normalized trial state
\Psi\in\mathcal{F}
, satisfying the hard-core condition, with particle density
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle/|\Lambda|
close to
\rho>0
and energy density
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{K}\Psi\rangle/|\Lambda|
close to the Lee-Huang-Yang prediction, in the limit of small
\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3}\ll 1
. To reach this goal, we consider first a Bose-Einstein condensate with density
0<\rho_{0}<\rho
to be fixed later on. The condensate is modeled by a coherent state
W(\rho_{0})\Omega
, generated by the unitary Weyl operator

W(\rho_{0})=e^{a^{*}(\sqrt{\rho_{0}})-a(\sqrt{\rho_{0}})}=e^{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\int dx\,(a_{x}^{*}-a_{x})}\,

acting on the vacuum vector
\Omega=\{1,0,0,\dots\}
. The action of
W(\rho_{0})
on creation and annihilation operators is determined by

W(\rho_{0})^{*}a_{x}W(\rho_{0})=a_{x}+\sqrt{\rho_{0}},\qquad W(\rho_{0})^{*}a_{x}^{*}W(\rho_{0})=a_{x}^{*}+\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\,.

(2.3)

Hence, the coherent state
W(\rho_{0})\Omega
is an eigenvector of all annihilation operators, with

a_{x}W(\rho_{0})\Omega=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}\,W(\rho_{0})\Omega

and its expected number of particles is given by

\langle W(\rho_{0})\Omega,\mathcal{N}W(\rho_{0})\Omega\rangle=\rho_{0}L^{3}=:N_{0}\,.

More precisely, the number of particles in the coherent state

W(\rho_{0})\Omega=e^{-N_{0}/2}\Big\{1,\sqrt{\rho_{0}},\dots\frac{\rho_{0}^{n/2}}{\sqrt{n!}},\dots\Big\}

is Poisson distributed, with average and variance given by
N_{0}
.

The coherent state
W(\rho_{0})\Omega
describes a completely uncorrelated state. To make sure that our trial state satisfies the hard-core condition (
1.2
) and that its energy is close to the true ground state energy, we need to add correlations. To this end, we consider the solution

f(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1-\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|},&\quad\text{if }|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}\\
0,&\quad\text{if }|x|<\mathfrak{a}\end{array}\right.

(2.4)

of the zero energy scattering equation

-\Delta f(x)=0,\quad\text{for }|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}

with the hard-core condition
f(x)=0
for
|x|=\mathfrak{a}
and the boundary condition
f(x)\to 1
, as
|x|\to\infty
. We fix
\ell=\mathfrak{a}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\delta}
, for some
\delta>0
which will be chosen small enough, and we define

\begin{split}f_{\ell}(x)&=\chi_{\ell}(x)f(x)+(1-\chi_{\ell}(x))\\
&=1-\chi_{\ell}(x)(1-f(x))=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1-\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|}\chi_{\ell}(x),&\text{if }|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}\\
0,&\text{if }|x|\leq\mathfrak{a}\end{array}\right.\end{split}

(2.5)

where
\chi_{\ell}(x)=\chi(x/\ell)
and
\chi\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\mathbb{R}^{3})
, with
\chi(x)=1
for
|x|\leq 2
,
\chi(x)=0
for
|x|>4
. By definition,
f_{\ell}
describes the two-particle correlations generated by the hard-core potential, up to the length scale
\ell=\mathfrak{a}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\delta}
, much larger than the scattering length
\mathfrak{a}
but much smaller than the healing length
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
. In the next lemma, we collect some important properties of
f_{\ell}
.

Lemma 2.1
.

Let
f_{\ell}
be as defined in (
2.5
) and

\omega_{\ell}(x)=1-f_{\ell}(x)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}1&\quad\text{if }|x|<\mathfrak{a}\\
\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|}\chi_{\ell}(x)&\quad\text{if }|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}\,.\end{array}\right.

Then
0\leq f_{\ell}(x),\omega_{\ell}(x)\leq 1
and, for
|x|>\mathfrak{a}
,

|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|=|\nabla\omega_{\ell}(x)|\leq C\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|^{2}}\chi_{\ell}(x)\,.

Moreover,

\Big|\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x-4\pi\mathfrak{a}\Big|\leq C\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\ell}\,.

(2.6)

Proof.

The bounds on
f_{\ell},\omega_{\ell}
follow directly from (
2.5
). To prove (
2.6
), we observe that

\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}dx=\int|\nabla\omega_{\ell}(x)|^{2}dx

and we compute

\nabla\omega_{\ell}(x)=-\frac{\mathfrak{a}x}{|x|^{3}}\chi(x/\ell)+\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{\ell|x|}\nabla\chi(x/\ell)\,.

Hence

\begin{split}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}dx=\;&\int_{|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}\chi^{2}(x/\ell)\,dx\\
&+\int_{|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{|x|^{2}\ell^{2}}|\nabla\chi(x/\ell)|^{2}dx-2\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\ell}\int\frac{x}{|x|^{4}}\cdot\nabla\chi(x/\ell)\chi(x/\ell)dx\,.\end{split}

With

\int_{|x|\geq\mathfrak{a}}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx=4\pi\mathfrak{a}

and

\begin{split}\int_{|x|\geq 2\ell}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{|x|^{4}}dx\,,\;\;\int_{2\ell\leq|x|\leq 4\ell}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\ell^{2}|x|^{2}}dx\,,\;\int_{2\ell\leq|x|\leq 4\ell}\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\ell|x|^{3}}dx&\leq C\frac{\mathfrak{a}^{2}}{\ell}\end{split}

we obtain (
2.6
).
∎

With
f_{\ell}
, we introduce the operator
J:\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
, setting

(J\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})=\prod_{i<j}^{n}f_{\ell}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Phi^{(n)}(x_{1},\ldots,x_{n})\,.

(2.7)

For
x\in\Lambda
, it is also convenient to define
J(x):\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{F}
by

(J(x)\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})=\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})\Phi^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\,.

With (
2.1
), we have, for
\Phi\in\mathcal{F}
,

\begin{split}(a_{x}J\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})&=\sqrt{n+1}\,(J\Phi)^{(n+1)}(x,x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\\
&=\sqrt{n+1}\,\prod_{k=1}^{n}f_{\ell}(x-x_{k})\prod_{1\leq i<j\leq n}f_{\ell}(x_{i}-x_{j})\Phi^{(n+1)}(x,x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\\
&=(J(x)Ja_{x}\Phi)^{(n)}(x_{1},\dots,x_{n})\,.\end{split}

In other words,

a_{x}J=J(x)Ja_{x},\qquad Ja_{x}^{*}=a_{x}^{*}J(x)J

(2.8)

for all
x\in\Lambda
. Similarly, we obtain the pull-through formulas

a_{y}J(x)=f_{\ell}(x-y)J(x)a_{y},\qquad J(x)a_{y}^{*}=f_{\ell}(x-y)a_{y}^{*}J(x)

(2.9)

for all
x,y\in\Lambda
. We find
0\leq J(x),J\leq 1
for all
x\in\Lambda
. Recalling that
\omega_{\ell}=1-f_{\ell}
and noting

1-\prod_{j=1}^{n}f_{\ell}(x-x_{j})\leq\sum_{j=1}^{n}(1-f_{\ell}(x-x_{j}))=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\omega_{\ell}(x-x_{j})

we obtain the useful bound

0\leq 1-J(x)\leq d\Gamma((\omega_{\ell})_{x})=\int dy\,\omega_{\ell}(x-y)a_{y}^{*}a_{y}\,.

(2.10)

The Jastrow factor generated by
J
models correlations among particles, up to the scale
\ell=\mathfrak{a}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\delta}
. To resolve the energy to Lee-Huang-Yang precision, we need to describe correlations up to larger distances, comparable with the healing length
(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
. To achieve this goal, we introduce a second length scale
\ell_{0}=(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\varepsilon}\gg\ell
, for some
\varepsilon>0
, which will also be chosen small enough. We are going to implement correlations on scales
\ell\lesssim|x|\lesssim\ell_{0}
through a Bogoliubov transformation

T=\exp\left[\frac{1}{2}\int dxdy\,\eta(x-y)(a_{x}^{*}a_{y}^{*}-a_{x}a_{y})\right]=\exp\Big[\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\widehat{\eta}_{p}\big(\hat{a}_{p}^{*}\hat{a}_{-p}^{*}-\hat{a}_{p}\hat{a}_{-p}\big)\Big]

(2.11)

with an appropriate kernel
\eta
, with Fourier coefficients
\widehat{\eta}_{p}
, for
p\in\Lambda^{*}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}/L
. Here, we define the Fourier coefficients of
h\in L^{1}(\Lambda)
by

\widehat{h}_{p}=\int dx\,h(x)\,e^{-ip\cdot x}

for all
p\in\Lambda^{*}
, so that

h(x)=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\widehat{h}_{p}\,e^{ip\cdot x}

and we have the identity

\langle h,g\rangle=\int dx\,\bar{h}(x)g(x)=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\overline{\widehat{h}}_{p}\widehat{g}_{p}\,.

(2.12)

In (
2.11
), we also introduced the momentum-space creation and annihilation operators
\hat{a}_{p}=a(\varphi_{p})
,
\hat{a}^{*}_{p}=a^{*}(\varphi_{p})
, with the normalized plane wave
\varphi_{p}(x)=e^{ip\cdot x}/|\Lambda|^{1/2}
. The action of the unitary operator
T
on the operators
\hat{a}_{p},\hat{a}_{p}^{*}
is explicitly given by

\begin{split}T^{*}\hat{a}_{p}T&=\widehat{\gamma}_{p}\hat{a}_{p}+\widehat{\sigma}_{p}\hat{a}_{-p}^{*},\qquad T^{*}\hat{a}_{p}^{*}T=\widehat{\gamma}_{p}\hat{a}_{p}^{*}+\widehat{\sigma}_{p}\hat{a}_{-p}\end{split}

(2.13)

with the notation
\widehat{\gamma}_{p}=\cosh\widehat{\eta}_{p}
,
\widehat{\sigma}_{p}=\sinh\widehat{\eta}_{p}
, for
p\in\Lambda^{*}
.

The choice of the kernel
\eta
should achieve two goals. On short length scales
\ell\lesssim|x|\ll\ell_{0}
,
\eta
should correct the function
f_{\ell}
used in the definition (
2.7
) of the Jastrow factor and transform it approximately into
f_{\ell_{0}}
, which describes correlations up to the scale
\ell_{0}
. To this end, we have to make sure that

1+\frac{1}{\rho}\eta(x)\simeq\frac{f_{\ell_{0}}(x)}{f_{\ell}(x)}

or, equivalently,

\eta(x)\simeq\rho\frac{\omega_{\ell}(x)-\omega_{\ell_{0}}(x)}{f_{\ell}(x)}

(2.14)

for
\ell\lesssim|x|\ll\ell_{0}
. On the other hand, on length scales
|x|\simeq(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}
comparable with the healing length, the action of
T
should diagonalize the emerging quadratic Hamiltonian

H_{\text{eff}}=\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}(p^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p))\hat{a}_{p}^{*}\hat{a}_{p}+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)\big(\hat{a}_{p}^{*}\hat{a}_{-p}^{*}+\hat{a}_{p}\hat{a}_{-p}\big)

(2.15)

with an effective potential
V_{\text{eff}}
, having the same scattering length
\mathfrak{a}
as the original interaction, but no hard-core. The fact that the second order corrections to the ground state energy density can be obtained by diagonalizing an Hamiltonian of the form (
2.15
) originates in Bogoliubov’s heuristic argument
[
9
]
. The validity of this heuristics has been rigorously established in recent years in the Gross–Pitaevskii regime, see e.g.
[
8
,
26
,
12
]
.
In fact, it is convenient to define
V_{\text{eff}}
using the solution (
2.4
) of the zero-energy scattering equation. On
\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we find
-2\Delta f(x)=2\delta(|x|-\mathfrak{a})/\mathfrak{a}
, with the Fourier transform

\int\frac{2}{\mathfrak{a}}\delta(|x|-\mathfrak{a})e^{-ip\cdot x}dx=8\pi\frac{\sin|p|\mathfrak{a}}{|p|}\,.

For
p\in\Lambda^{*}
, we define

\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)=8\pi\frac{\sin|p|\mathfrak{a}}{|p|}

(2.16)

and we denote by
V_{\text{eff}}(x)
the periodic function on
\Lambda
, with Fourier coefficients (
2.16
). From Bogoliubov theory, to diagonalize (
2.15
) we must have

\tanh(2\widehat{\eta}_{p})\simeq-\frac{\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)}{p^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)}

(2.17)

for
|p|\simeq\rho^{1/2}
, which implies that
\sinh\widehat{\eta}_{p}\simeq\widehat{s}_{p}
,
\cosh\widehat{\eta}_{p}\simeq\sqrt{1+\widehat{s}^{2}_{p}}
, with

\begin{split}\widehat{s}_{p}&=-\frac{p^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)-\sqrt{|p|^{4}+2p^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}^{2}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)^{2}-\Big(p^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)-\sqrt{|p|^{4}+2p^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(p)}\Big)^{2}}}\,.\end{split}

(2.18)

Instead of choosing the kernel
\eta
, it is convenient to set

\widetilde{\sigma}(x)=\frac{\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))}{f_{\ell}(x)}\big[\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}(x)+s(x)\big]

(2.19)

with
s(x)
defined through the Fourier coefficients (
2.18
), for
p\not=0
(and
\widehat{s}(0)=0
). By definition,
\tilde{\sigma}
is supported on
\ell\leq|x|\leq 2\ell_{0}
. Moreover, for
|k|\gg(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{1/2}
, (
2.18
) implies that

\widehat{s}(k)\simeq-\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)/2k^{2}\simeq-\rho_{0}\,\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k),

which is consistent with (
2.14
). Furthermore, for
\ell\ll|x|\lesssim\ell_{0}
, we have
\omega_{\ell}(x)=0
and
\tilde{\sigma}(x)=s(x)
, which is consistent with (
2.17
). To stay orthogonal to the condensate, it is useful to modify
\tilde{\sigma}
, defining instead

\sigma(x)=\tilde{\sigma}(x)-\ell_{0}^{-3}\Big(\int{\tilde{\sigma}}(z)dz\Big)\varphi(x/\ell_{0})

(2.20)

with
\varphi
smooth and compactly supported, with
\int\varphi(x)dx=1
and such that
\varphi(x)=1/2
for
|x|\leq 1
and
\varphi(x)=0
for
|x|>2
. Indeed, (
2.20
) guarantees that
\widehat{\sigma}_{0}=0
.

With (
2.20
), we can now define

\widehat{\eta}_{p}=\sinh^{-1}(\widehat{\sigma}_{p})\,.

(2.21)

We will make use of the Bogoliubov transformation (
2.11
), with kernel
\eta
defined through its Fourier coefficients (
2.21
). The action of
T
on creation and annihilation operators is determined by (
2.13
), with
\widehat{\sigma}_{p}
being the Fourier coefficients of the kernel (
2.20
) and with
\widehat{\gamma}_{p}=\cosh\widehat{\eta}_{p}
. Some important properties of these kernels (and some other kernels that will play an important role in our analysis) are collected in the following lemma, whose proof is deferred to Appendix
A
.

Lemma 2.2
.

The coefficients
\widehat{s}_{k}
, defined in (
2.18
), satisfy

|\widehat{s}_{k}|\leq C\min\Big\{\frac{\rho^{1/4}}{|k|^{1/2}},\frac{\rho}{k^{2}}\Big\}

(2.22)

for all
k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}
. For the function
s:\Lambda\to\mathbb{R}
with Fourier coefficients
\widehat{s}_{k}
we find the pointwise bounds

|s(x)|\leq C\frac{\rho}{|x|}\min\Big\{1,\frac{1}{(\rho^{1/2}|x|)^{3/2}}\Big\},\qquad|\nabla s(x)|\leq C\frac{\rho|\hskip-1.42271pt\log\rho|}{x^{2}}

(2.23)

for all
|x|\geq 2\mathfrak{a}
. Moreover

\int_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}|s(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\ell_{0}^{-2}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}},\;\int_{2\mathfrak{a}\leq|x|\leq 2\ell}|s(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\ell\rho^{2},\;\int_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}|\nabla s(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\ell_{0}^{-4}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

(2.24)

Let now
\zeta\in\{\tilde{\sigma},\sigma,\gamma-\mathbbm{1},\mathbbm{1}-\gamma^{-1},\gamma^{-1}*\sigma\}
. Then we have

\|\zeta\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\rho^{\frac{3}{2}},\qquad\|\zeta\|_{\infty}\leq C\,\frac{\rho}{\ell},\qquad\|\zeta\|_{1}\leq C\big(\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\ell_{0}\big)^{3},

(2.25)

and also

\|\nabla\zeta\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\rho^{2},\qquad\|\nabla\zeta\|_{\infty}\leq C\,\frac{\rho}{\ell^{2}}\,,

(2.26)

and, for any
m\in\mathbb{N}
, the pointwise decay estimate

|\zeta(x)|\leq C\,\frac{\rho}{\ell}\Big(\frac{\rho^{\frac{1}{4}}\ell_{0}^{\frac{3}{2}}}{|x|}\Big)^{m}\leq C\,\frac{\rho}{\ell}\,\frac{1}{\big(\rho^{1/2+3\varepsilon/2}|x|\big)^{m}}\,.

(2.27)

For
\zeta\in\{\tilde{\sigma},\sigma\}
, we get stronger estimates. In particular

\int_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}\big[|\tilde{\sigma}(x)|^{2}+|\sigma(x)|^{2}\big]dx\leq C\ell_{0}^{-2}\rho^{\frac{1}{2}},\qquad\int_{|x|\leq 2\ell}\big[|\tilde{\sigma}(x)|^{2}+|\sigma(x)|^{2}\big]dx\leq C\ell\rho^{2},

(2.28)

and

\|\sigma\|_{1}\leq 2\|\tilde{\sigma}\|_{1}\leq C\big(\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\ell_{0}\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

(2.29)

Additionally, we get the pointwise bounds

|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}(x)|,|\nabla\sigma(x)|\leq C\frac{\rho|\hskip-0.85355pt\log\rho|}{x^{2}}

(2.30)

for all
x\in\Lambda
, which imply

\|\nabla\sigma\|_{p},\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{p}\leq C\rho|\hskip-0.85355pt\log\rho|\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ell^{3/p-2}\quad\text{if }p>3/2\\
\ell_{0}^{3/p-2}\quad\text{if }p<3/2\end{array}\right.

(2.31)

and

\|\nabla(\gamma^{-1}*\sigma)\|_{p}\leq C(\rho^{1/2}\ell_{0})^{3}\rho|\hskip-0.85355pt\log\rho|\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\ell^{3/p-2}&\text{if }p>3/2\\
\ell_{0}^{3/p-2}&\text{if }p<3/2\end{array}\right.

(2.32)

for all
1\leq p\leq\infty
.

We are now ready to define our trial state. For
0<\rho_{0}<\rho
to be fixed later
we set

\Psi=Z^{-1}JW(\rho_{0})T\Omega\in\mathcal{F}

(2.33)

where
Z=\|JW(\rho_{0})T\Omega\|_{\mathcal{F}}
is a normalization constant, ensuring that
\|\Psi\|_{\mathcal{F}}=1
. Our analysis is based on the observation that the action of annihilation operators on the trial state
\Psi
can be expressed through a simple identity.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
\Psi
be defined as in (
2.33
). Then, for every
x\in\Lambda
, we have

a_{x}\Psi=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}J(x)\Psi+J(x)\int dy\,(\gamma^{-1}*\sigma)(x-y)a^{*}_{y}J(y)\Psi.

(2.34)

Proof.

With (
2.8
), we find, introducing the notation
\tau_{x}(y)=\tau(x-y)
, for
\tau=\gamma,\sigma
,

\begin{split}a_{x}\Psi&=Z^{-1}a_{x}JW(\rho_{0})T\Omega\\
&=Z^{-1}J(x)Ja_{x}W(\rho_{0})T\Omega\\
&=Z^{-1}J(x)JW(\rho_{0})T(a(\gamma_{x})+a^{*}(\sigma_{x})+\sqrt{\rho_{0}})\Omega\\
&=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}J(x)\Psi+Z^{-1}J(x)Ja^{*}(\gamma^{-1}*\sigma_{x})W(\rho_{0})T\Omega\end{split}

using (
2.3
) and the explicit action (
2.13
) of the Bogoliubov transform
T
, which translates to

T^{*}a_{x}T=a(\gamma_{x})+a^{*}(\sigma_{x}),\qquad T^{*}a_{x}^{*}T=a^{*}(\gamma_{x})+a(\sigma_{x})

in position space. Hence, applying again (
2.8
), we arrive at

\begin{split}a_{x}\Psi&=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}J(x)\Psi+Z^{-1}\int dy\,(\gamma^{-1}*\sigma)(x-y)J(x)Ja_{y}^{*}W(\rho_{0})T\Omega\\
&=\sqrt{\rho_{0}}J(x)\Psi+\int dy\,(\gamma^{-1}*\sigma)(x-y)J(x)a_{y}^{*}J(y)\Psi\,.\end{split}

∎

In the next sections, we will use the identity (
2.34
) to derive precise estimates on the number of particles and on the energy in the trial state
\Psi
, as stated in the following two propositions.

Proposition 2.4
.

We have

\big|\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle-(\rho_{0}+\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2})L^{3}\big|\leq C\rho^{7/4-11\varepsilon-\delta}L^{3}

(2.35)

if
\varepsilon,\delta>0
are small enough.

Proposition 2.5
.

Let

\begin{split}E_{\rho}=\;&\rho_{0}^{2}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x+2\rho_{0}\|\sigma\|^{2}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x+\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}|\sigma(x)|^{2}dx\\
&+2\int f_{\ell}(x)\nabla f_{\ell}(x)\sigma(x)\nabla\sigma(x)dx+\int|f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}|\nabla\sigma(x)|^{2}{d}x\\
&+2\rho_{0}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}\big[(\gamma\ast\sigma)(x)+(\sigma\ast\sigma)(x)\big]{d}x\\
&+2\rho_{0}\int f_{\ell}(x)\nabla f_{\ell}(x)\cdot\nabla\sigma(x){d}x\,.\end{split}

(2.36)

Then, recalling (
2.2
), there exists a constant
C>0
such that

L^{-3}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{K}\Psi\rangle\leq E_{\rho}+C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

(2.37)

for all
0<\delta<\varepsilon
with
\varepsilon
small enough.

3
Proof of Theorem
1.1

In the following we show how Theorem
1.1
follows from Proposition
2.4
and Proposition
2.5
.

To this end, we fix the density
0<\rho_{0}<\rho
of the condensate, requiring that

\rho+C_{1}\rho^{7/4-11\varepsilon-\delta}\leq\rho_{0}+\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}\leq\rho+C_{2}\rho^{7/4-11\varepsilon-\delta}

(3.1)

for some constants
0<C_{1}<C_{2}
. This is possible because
\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}\simeq\rho_{0}^{3/2}\gg\rho^{7/4-11\varepsilon-\delta}
, if
\varepsilon,\delta>0
are small enough. Choosing the constant
C_{1}>0
sufficiently large, (
2.35
) implies that
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle\geq\rho L^{3}
.

Next, we focus on the energy density
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{K}\Psi\rangle/L^{3}
of the trial state
\Psi
. In the next lemma, we identify the major contributions to
E_{\rho}
.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
E_{\rho}
defined in (
2.36
). Recall the choices
\ell=\mathfrak{a}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\delta}
and
\ell_{0}=(\rho\mathfrak{a})^{-1/2}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{-\varepsilon}
. We set

\begin{split}\vartheta(x)=&\;-\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}(x)+f_{\ell}(x)\tilde{\sigma}(x)=-\rho_{0}\chi_{\ell}(2x)\omega_{\ell}(x)+\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))s(x)\end{split}

(3.2)

with
\tilde{\sigma}
defined as in (
2.19
) and we define

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}=\|\nabla\vartheta\|_{2}^{2}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\big((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast s\big)(0)

(3.3)

with
g(x)
defined through its Fourier coefficients
\widehat{g}(k)=\sqrt{1+\widehat{s}^{2}(k)}
. Then,

|E_{\rho}-\widetilde{E}_{\rho}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

(3.4)

for all
0<\delta<\varepsilon/2
, with
\varepsilon>0
small enough.

Proof.

Decomposing
\gamma=(\gamma-\mathbbm{1})+\mathbbm{1}
we can write

\begin{split}E_{\rho}=\;&\rho_{0}^{2}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x+\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}|\sigma(x)|^{2}dx\\
&+2\int f_{\ell}(x)\sigma(x)\nabla f_{\ell}(x)\cdot\nabla\sigma(x)dx+\int|f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}|\nabla\sigma(x)|^{2}dx\\
&+2\rho_{0}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}\sigma(x){d}x+2\rho_{0}\int f_{\ell}(x)\nabla f_{\ell}(x)\cdot\nabla\sigma(x){d}x\\
&+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{R}_{i}\end{split}

(3.5)

where we introduced the notations

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{1}=\;&2\rho_{0}\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x-8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}\\
\mathcal{R}_{2}=\;&2\rho_{0}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(x){d}x-8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\\
\mathcal{R}_{3}=\;&2\rho_{0}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}(\sigma\ast\sigma)(x){d}x-8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}\,.\end{split}

Recognizing that the first three lines on the r.h.s. of (
3.5
) form a perfect square and recalling the definition (
3.2
), we conclude that

\begin{split}E_{\rho}=\;&\|\nabla\{-\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}+f_{\ell}\sigma\}\|_{2}^{2}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{R}_{i}\,\\
=\;&\|\nabla\vartheta\|_{2}^{2}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\|s\|_{2}^{2}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)+\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathcal{R}_{i}\end{split}

(3.6)

with

\mathcal{R}_{4}=\|\nabla\{-\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}+f_{\ell}\sigma\}\|_{2}^{2}-\|\nabla\{-\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}+f_{\ell}\tilde{\sigma}\}\|_{2}^{2}\,.

To complete the proof of the lemma, we show that
|\mathcal{R}_{i}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon/2}
, for all
i=1,\ldots,4
, if
0<\delta<\varepsilon
and
\varepsilon>0
is small enough.

We focus first on
\mathcal{R}_{1}
. We split
\mathcal{R}_{1}=\mathcal{R}_{11}+\mathcal{R}_{12}
with

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{11}=\;&2\rho_{0}\big(\|\sigma\|^{2}_{2}-\|s\|^{2}_{2}\big)\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x\\
\mathcal{R}_{12}=\;&2\rho_{0}\|s\|^{2}_{2}\Big(\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}{d}x-4\pi\mathfrak{a}\Big)\,.\end{split}

With (
2.6
)
we have

|\mathcal{R}_{12}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}\,.

Next, we claim that
|\mathcal{R}_{11}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}
. To prove this bound, we show that

\|\sigma-s\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/4+\varepsilon}

(3.7)

which, together with Lemma
2.2
, implies that

\big|\|\sigma\|_{2}^{2}-\|s\|_{2}^{2}\big|\leq C\big(\|\sigma\|_{2}+\|s\|_{2}\big)\|\sigma-s\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/2+\varepsilon}

(3.8)

and therefore that
|\mathcal{R}_{11}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}
(the bound (
2.22
) implies, in particular, that
\|s\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/4}
). To prove (
3.7
), we first estimate
\|\tilde{\sigma}-s\|_{2}
, with
\tilde{\sigma}
as defined in (
2.19
). To this end, we decompose
\tilde{\sigma}(x)=\chi_{\ell}(x/2)\tilde{\sigma}(x)+(1-\chi_{\ell}(x/2))\tilde{\sigma}(x)
. Observing that
f_{\ell}(x)=1
(and thus
\omega_{\ell}(x)=0
) on the support of
1-\chi_{\ell}(x/2)
, we find

\begin{split}\tilde{\sigma}(x)-s(x)=\;&\frac{\chi_{\ell}(x/2)(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))}{f_{\ell}(x)}\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell}(x)+\frac{\chi_{\ell}(x/2)(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))}{f_{\ell}(x)}s(x)\\
&-\chi_{\ell}(x/2)s(x)-(1-\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x))s(x)\,.\end{split}

Since
f_{\ell}(x)\geq C>0
, on the support of
1-\chi_{\ell}(2x)
, we arrive at

\|\tilde{\sigma}-s\|_{2}\leq C\rho\|\omega_{\ell}\|_{2}+C\|\chi_{\ell}(\cdot/2)s\|_{2}+\|(1-\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))s\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/4+\varepsilon}

if
\varepsilon,\delta>0
are small enough. Here, we estimated
\|\omega_{\ell}\|_{2}\leq C\ell^{1/2}
and we applied Lemma
2.2
to bound the other terms. With

\|\tilde{\sigma}-\sigma\|_{2}\leq\ell_{0}^{-3}\|\tilde{\sigma}\|_{1}\|\varphi(\cdot/\ell_{0})\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/4+\varepsilon}

we conclude that
\|\sigma-s\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/4+\varepsilon}
(using
\|\tilde{\sigma}\|_{1}\lesssim\rho^{-\varepsilon/2}
, from Lemma
2.2
).

We switch to
\mathcal{R}_{2}
. We first rewrite
\mathcal{R}_{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{R}_{2i}
with

\begin{split}\mathcal{R}_{21}=\;&2\rho_{0}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}\big(((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(x)-((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\big){d}x\\
\mathcal{R}_{22}=\;&2\rho_{0}((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\big(\|\nabla f_{\ell}\|^{2}-4\pi\mathfrak{a}\big)\\
\mathcal{R}_{23}=\;&8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\big(((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)-((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\big)\,.\\
\end{split}

To bound
\mathcal{R}_{21}
we use (recalling (
2.12
))

\big|((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(x)-((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\big|\leq C|x|\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}|p||\widehat{(\gamma-\mathbbm{1})}(p)||\widehat{\sigma}_{p}|\leq C|x|\|\gamma-\mathbbm{1}\|_{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{2}\,.

With Lemma
2.1
and Lemma
2.2
, we find

|\mathcal{R}_{21}|\leq C\rho\|\gamma-\mathbbm{1}\|_{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{2}\int|\nabla f_{\ell}(x)|^{2}|x|{d}x\leq C\rho^{11/4-\delta}\,.

On the other hand, Lemma
2.1
and the bound
((\gamma-\mathbbm{1})\ast\sigma)(0)\leq\|\gamma-\mathbbm{1}\|_{2}\|\sigma\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/2}
yield

\mathcal{R}_{22}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}\,.

Finally, to bound
\mathcal{R}_{23}
we write

((\gamma-g)\ast\sigma)(0)=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}(\widehat{\gamma}_{p}-\widehat{g}_{p})\widehat{\sigma}_{p}=\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}\Big(\sqrt{1+\widehat{\sigma}_{p}^{2}}-\sqrt{1+\widehat{s}^{2}_{p}}\,\Big)\widehat{\sigma}_{p}\,.

Using the fact that
x\to\sqrt{1+x^{2}}
is Lipshitz and (
3.7
), we find

\big|((\gamma-g)\ast\sigma)(0)\big|\leq\frac{C}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}|\widehat{\sigma}_{p}-\widehat{s}_{p}||\widehat{\sigma}_{p}|\leq C\|\sigma-s\|_{2}\|\sigma\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{3/2+\varepsilon}

implying that
|\mathcal{R}_{23}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}
.

The term
\mathcal{R}_{3}
can be bounded similarly to
\mathcal{R}_{2}
, using (
3.7
),
(\sigma\ast\sigma)(0)=\|\sigma\|^{2}_{2}
, and the bound

\big|(\sigma\ast\sigma)(x)-(\sigma\ast\sigma)(0)\big|\leq C|x|\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{p\in\Lambda^{*}}|p||\widehat{\sigma}_{p}|^{2}\leq C|x|\|\sigma\|_{2}\|\nabla\sigma\|_{2}\,.

Finally, we discuss the term
\mathcal{R}_{4}
, which can be written as

\mathcal{R}_{4}=\langle(\nabla f_{\ell})(\sigma-\tilde{\sigma})+f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),-2\rho_{0}\nabla\omega_{\ell}+(\nabla f_{\ell})\sigma+f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma)+(\nabla f_{\ell})\tilde{\sigma}+f_{\ell}(\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\rangle\,.

From Lemma
2.2
, we have

\int_{|x|\leq 4\ell}|\tilde{\sigma}(x)|^{2}dx,\int_{|x|\leq 4\ell}|\sigma(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\rho^{2}\ell\,.

(3.9)

Recalling that

\sigma(x)-\tilde{\sigma}(x)=-\ell_{0}^{-3}\varphi(x/\ell_{0})\Big(\int\tilde{\sigma}(z)dz\Big)

and (again from Lemma
2.2
) that
\|\tilde{\sigma}\|_{1}\leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon/2}
, we find

\int_{|x|\leq 4\ell}|\sigma(x)-\tilde{\sigma}(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon}\ell^{3}\ell_{0}^{-6}\leq C\rho^{3+5\varepsilon-3\delta}\leq C\rho^{3+2\varepsilon}

(3.10)

for all
\delta<\varepsilon
. Combining (
3.9
), (
3.10
) and recalling
\|\nabla f_{\ell}\|_{\infty},\|\nabla f_{\ell}\|_{2}\leq C
and also
\|\nabla\sigma\|_{2},\|\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\|_{2}\leq C\rho
, we obtain, by Cauchy-Schwarz,

\big|\langle(\nabla f_{\ell})(\sigma-\tilde{\sigma}),-2\rho_{0}\nabla\omega_{\ell}+(\nabla f_{\ell})\sigma+f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma)+(\nabla f_{\ell})\tilde{\sigma}+f_{\ell}(\nabla\tilde{\sigma})\rangle\big|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon-\delta}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

if
0<\delta<\varepsilon/2
and
\varepsilon>0
is small enough. Moreover,

\langle f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),-2\rho_{0}\nabla\omega_{\ell}+(\nabla f_{\ell})\sigma+(\nabla f_{\ell})\tilde{\sigma}\rangle=0

because the support of
\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}
is disjoint from the support of
\nabla f_{\ell}
. Finally, we estimate

\begin{split}|\langle f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),f_{\ell}\nabla\sigma\rangle|&\leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon/2}\ell_{0}^{-4}\Big|\int f^{2}_{\ell}(x)\nabla\sigma(x)\cdot\nabla\varphi(x/\ell_{0})dx\Big|\\
&\leq C\rho^{-\varepsilon/2}\ell_{0}^{-4}\int_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}|\nabla\sigma(x)||\nabla\varphi(x/\ell_{0})|dx\end{split}

because
\nabla\varphi(x)=0
, if
|x|\leq 1
. With Cauchy-Schwarz and Lemma
2.2
, we conclude that

|\langle f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),f_{\ell}\nabla\sigma\rangle|\leq C\ell_{0}^{-3}\rho^{1-\varepsilon/2}|\log\rho|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon/2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}.

The contribution
\langle f_{\ell}(\nabla\sigma-\nabla\tilde{\sigma}),f_{\ell}\nabla\tilde{\sigma}\rangle
can be handled similarly. This shows that
|\mathcal{R}_{4}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\delta}
and concludes the proof of the lemma.
∎

We focus now on the expression for
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
in (
3.3
) and show it can be bounded above by the Lee-Huang-Yang energy.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
\widetilde{E}_{\rho}
be defined as in (
3.3
). Then

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\Big]+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}

for all
\delta,\varepsilon>0
small enough.

Proof.

We start from the observation that

\|\nabla\vartheta\|^{2}\leq\|\nabla(\vartheta+{\rho_{0}}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\|^{2}+4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}_{0}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

(3.11)

To prove (
3.11
), we first observe, from the definition (
3.2
) of
\vartheta
, that

\vartheta(x)+\rho_{0}\omega(x)\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)=\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))(\rho_{0}\omega(x)+s(x))\,.

(3.12)

Since moreover

\big|(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))\Delta(\omega(x)\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x))\big|\lesssim\frac{1}{\ell_{0}^{2}|x|}\chi(\ell_{0}\leq|x|\leq 2\ell_{0})

because
\Delta\omega(x)=0
on the support of
1-\chi_{\ell}(2x)
, we conclude that

\begin{split}\big|\langle\nabla(\vartheta&+\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot)),\rho_{0}\nabla(\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\rangle\big|\\
&=\big|\langle\vartheta+\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot),\rho_{0}\Delta(\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\rangle\big|\\
&\leq C\rho\ell_{0}^{-2}\int_{\ell_{0}\leq|x|\leq 2\ell_{0}}\big(\rho_{0}\omega(x)+s(x))\frac{1}{|x|}dx\\
&\leq C\rho^{2}\ell_{0}^{-1}+C\rho\ell_{0}^{-2}\Big[\int_{|x|\leq 2\ell_{0}}\frac{1}{|x|^{2}}dx\Big]^{1/2}\Big[\int_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}|s(x)|^{2}dx\Big]^{1/2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.\end{split}

Writing
\vartheta=(\vartheta+\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))-\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot)
, we can therefore estimate

\|\nabla\vartheta\|^{2}\leq\|\nabla(\vartheta+\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\|^{2}+\rho_{0}^{2}\|\nabla(\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\|^{2}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

From
|\nabla(\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x))|\leq C\ell_{0}^{-1}\chi(|x|\leq 2\ell_{0})
, we also find

\begin{split}\rho_{0}^{2}\|\nabla(\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\|^{2}&\leq\rho_{0}^{2}\int_{|x|\leq 2\ell_{0}}|\nabla\omega(x)|^{2}dx+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\\
&\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}^{2}\big(1+C\mathfrak{a}/\ell_{0})+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}^{2}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\end{split}

and thus (
3.11
). Recalling the definition (
3.1
) of
\rho_{0}
and the bound (
3.8
), (
3.11
) yields

\|\nabla\vartheta\|^{2}+8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho\|s\|^{2}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\|\nabla(\vartheta+\rho_{0}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\|^{2}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

From (
3.3
), we arrive at

\begin{split}\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq\;&4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\left\|\nabla(s+{\rho_{0}}\omega_{\ell_{0}})\right\|^{2}\\
&+\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)\Big[\widehat{s}_{k}^{2}+\widehat{(g-\mathbbm{1})}_{k}\widehat{s}_{k}\Big]+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\end{split}

(3.13)

with the error terms

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{1}=\;

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla(\vartheta+{\rho_{0}}\omega\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))\right\|^{2}-\left\|\nabla(s+{\rho_{0}}\omega_{\ell_{0}})\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{2}=\;

\displaystyle 8\pi\mathfrak{a}{\rho_{0}}\|s\|^{2}-\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)\widehat{s}_{k}^{2}

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{3}=\;

\displaystyle 8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}\,((g-\mathbbm{1})\ast s)(0)-\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)\,\widehat{(g-\mathbbm{1})}(k)\widehat{s}_{k}\,.

Expanding the norm
\|\nabla(s+\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell_{0}})\|^{2}
in Fourier space, we further obtain

\begin{split}\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq\;&4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\Big[|k|^{2}\widehat{s}_{k}^{2}+{\rho_{0}}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)\big(\widehat{s}_{k}^{2}+\widehat{g}_{k}\widehat{s}_{k}\big)+\frac{\rho^{2}_{0}\widehat{V}^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}{4|k|^{2}}\Big]\\
&+\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\end{split}

(3.14)

with

\mathcal{E}_{4}=\frac{\rho_{0}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\big(2|k|^{2}\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k)-\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)\big)\widehat{s}_{k}\,,\quad\mathcal{E}_{5}\;=\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}\Big(|k|^{2}\widehat{\omega}^{2}_{\ell_{0}}(k)-\frac{\widehat{V}^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}{4|k|^{2}}\Big)\,.

With the definition (
2.18
), we have, for every
k\neq 0
,

\widehat{s}_{k}^{2}=\frac{|k|^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)-\sqrt{|k|^{4}+2|k|^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}}{2\sqrt{|k|^{4}+2|k|^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}}\,,\quad\widehat{g}_{k}\widehat{s}_{k}=\frac{-\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}{2\sqrt{|k|^{4}+2|k|^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}}\,.

Therefore, we can rewrite

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\frac{1}{2|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}}F(k)+\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}

(3.15)

with

F(k):=\sqrt{|k|^{4}+2|k|^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}-|k|^{2}-\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)+\frac{\rho^{2}_{0}\widehat{V}^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}{4|k|^{2}}\,.

(3.16)

Rewriting

F(k)=-\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}^{2}(k)}{\sqrt{|k|^{4}+2|k|^{2}\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}+|k|^{2}+\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)}+\frac{\rho^{2}_{0}\widehat{V}^{2}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)}{4|k|^{2}}

(3.17)

we immediately find
|F(k)|\leq C\rho^{2}/|k|^{2}
(recall
|\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)|\leq C
). Taylor expanding the square root in (
3.16
), we also obtain

|F(k)|\leq C\frac{\rho^{3}}{|k|^{4}}

for
|k|\geq C\rho_{0}^{1/2}
. With these two bounds, we conclude that the contributions to the sum of
F(k)
arising from
|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}
and from
|k|\geq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}
are negligible. Hence

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\frac{1}{2|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}:\\
\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}F(k)+\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

Replacing
\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)=8\pi\sin(|k|\mathfrak{a})/|k|
with
8\pi\mathfrak{a}
in (
3.16
), we define

G(k)=\sqrt{|k|^{4}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}|k|^{2}}-|k|^{2}-8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}+\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0})^{2}}{4|k|^{2}}\,.

Expressing also
G
in the form (
3.17
) and recalling that
|\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}|\leq C|k|^{2}
, we find

|F(k)-G(k)|\lesssim\frac{\rho^{2}|\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}|}{|k|^{2}}\leq C\rho^{2}

and therefore

\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}}|F(k)-G(k)|\leq C\rho^{7/2-3\varepsilon}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}

if
\varepsilon>0
is small enough. Hence

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}+\frac{1}{2|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}:\\
\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}G(k)+\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

Observing that

|\nabla G(k)|\leq C\frac{\rho^{2}}{|k|^{3}}\Big(1+C\frac{\rho}{k^{2}}\Big)\leq C\rho^{1/2-4\varepsilon}

on the annulus
\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}
, we conclude that, in every relevant cell of the lattice
\Lambda^{*}=2\pi\mathbb{Z}^{3}/L
, the variation of
G
is bounded by
C\rho^{1/2-4\varepsilon}/L
. This implies that, for sufficiently large
L
,

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}:\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}}G(k)\\
&\leq\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\rho_{0}^{1/2+\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{1/2-\varepsilon}}\Big[\sqrt{|k|^{4}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}|k|^{2}}-|k|^{2}-8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0}+\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho_{0})^{2}}{4|k|^{2}}\Big]dk+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\\
&=\frac{\rho_{0}^{5/2}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int_{\rho_{0}^{\varepsilon}\leq|k|\leq\rho_{0}^{-\varepsilon}}\Big[\sqrt{|k|^{4}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}|k|^{2}}-|k|^{2}-8\pi\mathfrak{a}+\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a})^{2}}{4|k|^{2}}\Big]dk+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\\
&\leq\frac{\rho_{0}^{5/2}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\int\Big[\sqrt{|k|^{4}+16\pi\mathfrak{a}|k|^{2}}-|k|^{2}-8\pi\mathfrak{a}+\frac{(8\pi\mathfrak{a})^{2}}{4|k|^{2}}\Big]dk+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\\
&=4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\cdot\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.\end{split}

In the last step, we computed the integral and we used the bound
|\rho-\rho_{0}|\leq C\rho^{3/2}
to replace
\rho_{0}^{5/2}
by
\rho^{5/2}
. We find

\widetilde{E}_{\rho}\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\left(1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\right)+\sum_{i=1}^{5}\mathcal{E}_{i}+C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

To conclude the proof of the lemma, we estimate the error terms
\mathcal{E}_{i},i=1,\dots,5
.

To bound
\mathcal{E}_{1}
, we define
Z(x)=\rho_{0}\omega_{\ell_{0}}(x)+s(x)
. Since
\omega(x)=\omega_{\ell_{0}}(x)
on the support of
\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)
, from (
3.12
) we can write

\vartheta(x)+\rho_{0}\omega(x)\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x)=(1-\chi_{\ell}(2x))Z(x)-(1-\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2x))Z(x)

(3.18)

which leads us to

\begin{split}|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\leq\;&\left\langle\nabla Z,\left[(1-\chi_{\ell}(2\cdot))^{2}-1\right]\nabla Z\right\rangle\\
&+2\big|\langle(1-\chi_{\ell}(2\cdot))\nabla Z,-\frac{2}{\ell}\nabla\chi(2\cdot/\ell)Z(x)-\nabla\{(1-\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))Z\}\rangle\big|\\
&+C\ell^{-2}\|\nabla\chi(2\cdot/\ell)Z\|^{2}+C\|\nabla\{(1-\chi_{\ell_{0}}(2\cdot))Z\}\|^{2}\end{split}

(3.19)

and implies that

|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\leq C\ell^{3}\|\nabla Z\|_{\infty}^{2}+C\ell^{2}\|\nabla Z\|_{\infty}\|Z\|_{\infty}+C\ell\|Z\|_{\infty}^{2}+C\|\nabla Z|_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}\|^{2}+C\ell_{0}^{-2}\|Z|_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}\|^{2}.

(3.20)

From Lemma
2.2
and noticing that

\int_{\ell_{0}\leq|x|\leq 4\ell_{0}}|\omega(x)|^{2}dx\leq C\ell_{0},\qquad\int_{\ell_{0}\leq|x|\leq 4\ell_{0}}|\nabla\omega(x)|^{2}\leq C\ell_{0}^{-1}

we arrive at

C\ell_{0}^{-2}\|Z|_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon},\qquad C\|\nabla Z|_{|x|\geq\ell_{0}}\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.

To bound the first three term on the r.h.s. of (
3.20
), we first estimate the Fourier coefficients
\widehat{Z}(k)
. From Lemma
2.2
, we have
|\widehat{Z}(k)|\leq C\rho/|k|^{2}
. Moreover, observing that

\Big|\widehat{s}_{k}+\frac{\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\rm{eff}}(k)}{2|k|^{2}}\Big|\leq\frac{C\rho_{0}^{2}(\widehat{V}_{\rm{eff}}(k))^{2}}{|k|^{4}}

for
|k|\gg\rho^{1/2}
, we can split

\widehat{Z}(k)=\widehat{Z}_{1}(k)+\widehat{Z}_{2}(k)
with

\widehat{Z}_{1}(k)=\rho_{0}\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k)-\frac{\rho_{0}\widehat{V}_{\rm{eff}}(k)}{2|k|^{2}}

(3.21)

and

\qquad\big|\widehat{Z}_{2}(k)\big|\leq C\rho^{2}\min\{|k|^{-4},|k|^{-6}\}

(3.22)

for
|k|\gg\rho^{1/2}
, since
|\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)|\leq C\min\{1,|k|^{-1}\}
. Writing

2k^{2}\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k)=2\int\min\Big\{1,\frac{\mathfrak{a}}{|x|}\Big\}\chi(x/\ell_{0})\Delta e^{-ik\cdot x}dx

and integrating by parts (with Gauss theorem), we find

2k^{2}\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k)-\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)=\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})

(3.23)

with
z_{1}(x)=4\mathfrak{a}\nabla\chi(x)\cdot x/|x|^{3}-2\mathfrak{a}\Delta\chi(x)/|x|
. This implies that
\widehat{Z}_{1}(k)=\rho_{0}\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})/2k^{2}
and therefore, introducing the notation

\|\widehat{h}\|_{p}^{p}=\frac{1}{L^{3}}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}}|\widehat{h}_{k}|^{p}

(3.24)

for
1\leq p<\infty
and
\|\widehat{h}\|_{\infty}=\sup_{k\in\Lambda^{*}}|\widehat{h}_{k}|
for sequences
\widehat{h}=\{\widehat{h}_{k}\}_{k\in\Lambda^{*}}
, that

\|\widehat{Z}_{1}\|_{1}\leq\frac{C\rho}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|\leq\ell_{0}^{-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{\|\widehat{z}_{1}\|_{\infty}}{k^{2}}+C\rho\ell_{0}^{-3/2}\Big(\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|\geq\ell_{0}^{-1}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k|^{4}}\Big)^{1/2}\leq C\rho\ell_{0}^{-1}

because
\|\widehat{z}_{1}(\ell_{0}\cdot)\|_{2}=\|z_{1}(x/\ell_{0})/\ell_{0}^{3}\|_{2}\leq C\ell_{0}^{-3/2}
. Since moreover

\|\widehat{Z}_{2}\|_{1}\leq\frac{C\rho}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|\leq K\rho^{1/2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{k^{2}}+\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|>K\rho^{1/2}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{|k|^{4}}\leq C\rho^{3/2}

we conclude that

\|Z\|_{\infty}\leq\|\widehat{Z}\|_{1}\leq\|\widehat{Z}_{1}\|_{1}+\|\widehat{Z}_{2}\|_{1}\leq C\rho^{3/2}\,.

Similarly, we can bound

\begin{split}\|\nabla Z\|_{\infty}&\leq\||\cdot|\widehat{Z}\|_{1}\leq\frac{C}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|\leq K\rho^{1/2}\end{subarray}}\frac{\rho}{|k|}+\frac{C}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}\\
|k|\geq K\rho^{1/2}\end{subarray}}\bigg[\frac{\rho|\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})|}{|k|}+|k||\widehat{Z}_{2}(k)|\bigg]\\
&\leq C\rho^{2}+C\rho\left[\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda^{*}}|\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})|^{2}|k|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\left[\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{|k|\geq K\rho^{1/2}}\frac{1}{|k|^{4}}\right]^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+\frac{C\rho^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{|k|\geq K\rho^{1/2}}\min\Big\{\frac{1}{|k|^{3}},\frac{1}{|k|^{5}}\Big\}\\
&\leq C\rho^{2}+C\rho^{3/4}\ell_{0}^{-5/2}+C\rho^{2}|\log\rho|\leq C\rho^{2}|\log\rho|\,.\end{split}

(3.25)

From (
3.20
), we conclude that
|\mathcal{E}_{1}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}
.

To bound
\mathcal{E}_{2}
we combine
|\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}|\leq C\min\{k^{2},1\}
with the estimate
|\widehat{s}_{k}|\leq C\rho/k^{2}
for all
k\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}
, from Lemma
2.2
. We obtain

\begin{split}|\mathcal{E}_{2}|&\leq\frac{C\rho}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}\\
|k|\leq\rho^{1/2-\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}|\widehat{V}_{\mathrm{eff}}(k)-8\pi\mathfrak{a}||\widehat{s}_{k}|^{2}+\frac{C\rho}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k\in\Lambda^{*}_{+}\\
|k|\geq\rho^{1/2-\varepsilon}\end{subarray}}|\widehat{s}_{k}|^{2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\end{split}

for all
\varepsilon>0
small enough. Similarly, with
|\widehat{(g-\mathbbm{1})}(k)|\leq|\widehat{s}_{k}|\leq\rho/k^{2}
we also find
|\mathcal{E}_{3}|\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}
.

Finally, we consider
\mathcal{E}_{4}
and
\mathcal{E}_{5}
. Recalling (
3.23
) and Lemma
2.2
, we find

|\mathcal{E}_{4}|=\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})\widehat{s}_{k}\Big|\leq C\rho\|\widehat{z}_{1}(\ell_{0}\cdot)\|_{2}\|\widehat{s}\|_{2}\leq C\rho^{7/4}\ell_{0}^{-3/2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+3\varepsilon/2}

since
\|\widehat{z}_{1}(\ell_{0}\cdot)\|_{2}\leq C\ell_{0}^{-3/2}
, and

\begin{split}|\mathcal{E}_{5}|&=\Big|\frac{\rho_{0}^{2}}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{k\in\Lambda_{+}^{*}}\frac{1}{4k^{2}}\widehat{z}_{1}(k\ell_{0})\big(2k^{2}\widehat{\omega}_{\ell_{0}}(k)+\widehat{V}_{\text{eff}}(k)\big)\Big|\\
&\leq C\rho^{2}\|\widehat{z}_{1}\|_{\infty}\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{|k|\leq\rho^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{k^{2}}+C\rho^{2}\|\widehat{z}_{1}(\ell_{0}\cdot)\|\Big[\frac{1}{|\Lambda|}\sum_{|k|\geq\rho^{1/2+\varepsilon}}\frac{1}{|k|^{4}}\Big]^{1/2}\\
&\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}+C\rho^{7/4-\varepsilon/2}\ell_{0}^{-3/2}\leq C\rho^{5/2+\varepsilon}\,.\end{split}

∎

Proposition
2.5
together with Lemmas
3.1
and
3.2
yield the upper bound

L^{-3}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{K}\Psi\rangle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\Big]+C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

(3.26)

if
0<\delta<\varepsilon/2
and
\varepsilon>0
is small enough. At the same time, we recall from (
3.1
) that
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle\geq\rho L^{3}
. To conclude the proof of Theorem
1.1
we use equivalence of ensembles, arguing similarly as in
[
5
, Appendix A]
. Starting with the periodic trial state
\Psi
we construct, following
[
5
, Lemma A.1]
, a new normalized Fock space vector
\Psi_{D}
on the box with size
L+2\mathcal{L}
for a fixed
\mathcal{L}>0
, satisfying Dirichlet boundary conditions and such that

\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{D}\rangle=\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle\geq\rho L^{3}

and

\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{K}\Psi_{D}\rangle\leq\langle\Psi,\mathcal{K}\Psi\rangle+\frac{C}{L\mathcal{L}}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle\,.

(3.27)

This implies

\frac{1}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{K}\Psi_{D}\rangle\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\Big]+C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

if
L>0
is large enough (the new error term can be absorbed in the contribution proportional to
\rho^{5/2+\delta}
). On the other hand, setting

e^{D}_{L}(\rho)=\frac{E^{D}(\rho L^{3},L)}{L^{3}}

with
E^{D}(N,L)
the ground state energy of
N
particles in a box of size
L
, with Dirichlet boundary conditions, we find

\begin{split}\frac{1}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{K}\Psi_{D}\rangle&=\frac{\mu}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{N}\Psi_{D}\rangle+\frac{1}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\langle\Psi_{D},(\mathcal{H}-\mu\mathcal{N})\Psi_{D}\rangle\\
&\geq\frac{\mu\rho}{(1+2\mathcal{L}/L)^{3}}+\sum_{m\geq 1}\left[e^{D}_{L+2\mathcal{L}}\Big(\frac{m}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\Big)-\mu\frac{m}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\right]\|\Psi_{D}^{(m)}\|^{2}\,.\end{split}

As explained in
[
5
, Eq. (A.16)]
, we can bound

e^{D}_{L+2\mathcal{L}}(\rho)\geq(1+\mathfrak{a}/L)^{3}\,e\big(\rho\,(1+\mathfrak{a}/\mathcal{L})^{-3}\big)

where we recall that
e(\rho)
is the ground state energy per unit volume, in the thermodynamics limit
N,L\to\infty
, at density
\rho
(the limiting energy density is independent of the boundary conditions). Thus, we obtain

\begin{split}&\frac{1}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}}\langle\Psi_{D},\mathcal{K}\Psi_{D}\rangle\\
&\geq\frac{\mu\rho}{(1+2\mathcal{L}/L)^{3}}\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt+(1+\mathfrak{a}/L)^{3}\sum_{m=1}^{CL^{3}}\left[e\big(\frac{m}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}(1+\mathfrak{a}/L)^{3}}\big)-\mu\frac{m}{(L+2\mathcal{L})^{3}(1+\mathfrak{a}/L)^{3}}\right]\|\Psi_{D}^{(m)}\|^{2}\\
&\geq\mu\rho(1+2\mathcal{L}/L)^{-3}-(1+\mathfrak{a}/L)^{3}e^{*}(\mu)\end{split}

with
e^{*}
the Legendre transform of
e
. From (
3.27
) and letting
L\to\infty
, we arrive at

4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\Big]+C\rho^{5/2+\delta}\geq\mu\rho-e^{*}(\mu)\,.

Since this is true for all
\mu>0
, and since the limiting energy density is known to be convex, we obtain

e(\rho)\leq 4\pi\mathfrak{a}\rho^{2}\Big[1+\frac{128}{15\sqrt{\pi}}(\rho\mathfrak{a}^{3})^{1/2}\Big]+C\rho^{5/2+\delta}

which concludes the proof of Theorem
1.1
.

4
A-priori bounds on trial state

In this section, we prove estimates on the local number of particles and of excitations in the trial state
\Psi
defined in (
2.33
). This will, first of all, allows us to show Prop.
2.4
. Moreover, it will give us a-priori control on
\Psi
, which will be crucial in the next section, to estimate the expectation of the kinetic energy and thus to prove Prop.
2.5
.

We recall the hierarchy of length scales:
\ell=C\rho^{-\delta}
,
\ell_{0}=C\rho^{-1/2-\varepsilon}
. For
w\in\Lambda
and
r>0
, we denote by

\mathcal{N}_{r}(w)=\int_{|y-w|\leq r}dy\,a_{y}^{*}a_{y}

(4.1)

the operator measuring the number of particles in a ball of radius
r
around
w
.
Moreover, introducing the distributions

\begin{split}b^{*}_{x}&=a^{*}_{x}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}=W(\rho_{0})a^{*}_{x}W(\rho_{0})^{*}\\
b_{x}&=a_{x}-\sqrt{\rho_{0}}=W(\rho_{0})a_{x}W(\rho_{0})^{*}\end{split}

(4.2)

creating and annihilating excitations of the Bose-Einstein condensate at a point
x\in\Lambda
, we define the operator

\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{r}(w)=\int_{|x-w|\leq r}dx\,b_{x}^{*}b_{x}\,.

(4.3)

In the next lemma, we estimate moments of the operators (
4.1
), (
4.3
), in the state
\Psi
, for
r
slightly larger than the length scale
\ell_{0}
(this restriction is needed, because we will use the fast decay (
2.27
) of the kernels
\sigma,\gamma^{-1}*\sigma
, at distances larger than
\ell_{0}
).

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
R=C\rho^{-1/2-3\varepsilon}
. For every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists
C=C(m)>0
such that

\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R}(w)\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{-m/2-12m\varepsilon}

(4.4)

and

\langle\Psi,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{m}_{R}(w)\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{-10m\varepsilon}

(4.5)

for every
w\in\Lambda
, if
\rho>0
and
\varepsilon,\delta>0
in the definition of the trial state
\Psi
are small enough (how small
\rho>0
needs to be depends on
m
).

Remark.
From the definition (
2.33
), it is clear that
\Psi
is translation invariant. Hence,
\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m}(w)\Psi\rangle
and
\langle\Psi,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{R}^{m}(w)\Psi\rangle
are actually independent of
w\in\Lambda
.

Remark.
The lemma implies that the expectation of the total number of particles
\mathcal{N}
and the total number of excitations
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}
are bounded by

\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}\Psi\rangle=\int dx\langle\Psi,a_{x}^{*}a_{x}\Psi\rangle\leq CR^{-3}\int dz\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}(z)\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{1-3\varepsilon}L^{3}

(4.6)

and, respectively,

\langle\Psi,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}\Psi\rangle=\int dx\langle\Psi,b_{x}^{*}b_{x}\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{3/2-\varepsilon}L^{3}\,.

(4.7)

Later, we will prove a more precise version of (
4.6
).

In order to show Lemma
4.1
, we use the following result, which allows us to estimate moments of the number of particles in balls of radius
R_{2}
by the corresponding moments in slightly smaller balls of radius
R_{1}
.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
m\in\mathbb{N}
. Then there exists
C=C(m)>0
such that

\langle\Phi,\mathcal{N}_{R_{2}}^{m}(w)\Phi\rangle\leq\left(1+C(R_{2}-R_{1})/R_{1}\right)\langle\Phi,\mathcal{N}_{R_{1}}^{m}(w)\Phi\rangle

(4.8)

for every translation invariant
\Phi\in\mathcal{F}
, all
0<R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{1}
and all
w\in\Lambda
.

Remark.
Since

W(\rho_{0})\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)W(\rho_{0})^{*}=\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{R}(w)\,,

(
4.8
) immediately implies the analogous bound with the local number of particles
\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)
replaced by the local number of excitations
\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}_{R}(w)
. In other words, for every
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\langle\Phi,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{m}_{R_{2}}(w)\Phi\rangle\leq\left(1+C(R_{2}-R_{1})/R_{1}\right)\langle\Phi,\widetilde{\mathcal{N}}^{m}_{R_{1}}(w)\Phi\rangle

(4.9)

for every
0<R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{1}
and for every translation invariant
\Phi\in\mathcal{F}(\Lambda_{L})
.

Proof.

For
m\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu
a non-negative
\sigma
-finite Borel measure on
\Lambda
, we set

F_{\mu}(R):=\int_{\Lambda}dy\;\left(\int_{|x-y|\leq R}d\mu(x)\right)^{m}\,.

We claim that

F_{\mu}(R_{2})\leq\big(1+C(R_{2}-R_{1})/R_{1}\big)F_{\mu}(R_{1})

(4.10)

for all
R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{1}
and for a constant
C>0
depending only on
m
.

From (
4.10
), the bound (
4.8
) follows by the observing that, on the
N
-particle sector of Fock space
\mathcal{F}
,
\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)
acts as multiplication with

\sum_{j=1}^{N}\chi(|x_{j}-w|\leq R)=\int_{|x-w|\leq R}d\mu(x)

where we defined the measure
\mu(x)=\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta(x_{j}-x)
. Therefore, working on sectors with fixed number of particles, (
4.10
) implies that

\int_{\Lambda}dw\,\langle\Phi,\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R_{2}}(w)\Phi\rangle\leq\big(1+C(R_{2}-R_{1})/R_{1}\big)\int_{\Lambda}dw\,\langle\Phi,\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R_{1}}(w)\Phi\rangle

which immediately yields (
4.8
), if we assume
\Phi
to be translation invariant.

To show (
4.10
), we use Fubini to write

\begin{split}F_{\mu}(R)&=\int d\mu(x_{1})\dots\int d\mu(x_{m})\int_{\Lambda}dy\,\prod_{j=1}^{m}\chi(|x_{j}-y|\leq R)\\
&=\int d\mu(x_{1})\dots\int d\mu(x_{m})\,K_{R}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\end{split}

where

K_{R}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=\text{Vol }\Big(B_{R}(x_{1})\cap B_{R}(x_{2})\cap\dots\cap B_{R}(x_{m})\Big)

with
\text{Vol}(A)
the Lebesgue measure of
A\subset\Lambda
. We observe that
K_{R}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})=0
, unless
|x_{i}-x_{j}|<2R
, for all
i,j\in\{1,\dots,m\}
.
Moreover, for
R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{2}
, we find
K_{R_{2}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\geq K_{R_{1}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})
by monotonicity and

K_{R_{2}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\leq K_{R_{1}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})+CR_{1}^{2}(R_{2}-R_{1})\prod_{j=2}^{m}\chi(|x_{1}-x_{j}|\leq 2R_{2})

(4.11)

for a constant
C>0
, depending only on
m
. To prove (
4.11
), it is enough to remark that, when we increase the radius of the
m
balls from
R_{1}
to
R_{2}
, the Lebesgue measure of their intersection can grow at most by

\frac{4}{3}\pi m(R_{2}^{3}-R_{1}^{3})\leq C(R_{2}-R_{1})R_{1}^{2}

(4.12)

if
R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{1}
. We conclude that

\begin{split}F_{\mu}(R_{2})&=\int d\mu(x_{1})\dots\int d\mu(x_{m})\,K_{R_{2}}(x_{1},\dots,x_{m})\\
&\leq F_{\mu}(R_{1})+C(R_{2}-R_{1})R_{1}^{2}\int d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{m})\end{split}

for all
R_{1}\leq R_{2}\leq 2R_{1}
. To bound the remaining integral, we find
k\in\mathbb{N}
points
\tilde{y}_{1},\dots,\tilde{y}_{k}\in B_{4}(0)
, with

B_{4}(0)\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}B_{1/2}(\tilde{y}_{j})\,.

Setting
y_{j}=R_{1}\tilde{y}_{j}
, for
j=1,\dots,k
, this implies that

B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})\subset\bigcup_{j=1}^{k}B_{R_{1}/2}(x_{1}+y_{j})

and therefore that

\begin{split}&\int d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{m})\\
&\leq\sum_{j_{2},\dots j_{m}=1}^{k}\int d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(x_{1}+y_{j_{2}})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(x_{1}+y_{j_{m}})}d\mu(x_{m})\\
&\leq\sum_{j_{2},\dots j_{m}=1}^{k}\frac{1}{|B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(0)|}\int dw\int_{B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(w)}d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(x_{1}+y_{j_{2}})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(x_{1}+y_{j_{m}})}d\mu(x_{m})\,,\end{split}

where in the last step we inserted a factor
\int dw\,\chi(|x_{1}-w|\leq R_{1}/2)/|B_{R_{1}/2}(0)|=1
and then we exchanged the
w
and the
x_{1}
integrals. We obtain

\begin{split}&\int d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{m})\\
&\leq\sum_{j_{2},\dots j_{m}=1}^{k}\frac{1}{|B_{\frac{R_{1}}{2}}(0)|}\int dw\int_{B_{R_{1}}(w)}d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{R_{1}}(w+y_{j_{2}})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{R_{1}}(w+y_{j_{m}})}d\mu(x_{m})\,.\end{split}

With the elementary inequality
a_{1}\dots a_{m}\lesssim C_{m}(a_{1}^{m}+\dots+a_{m}^{m})
, we conclude that

\int d\mu(x_{1})\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{2})\dots\int_{B_{4R_{1}}(x_{1})}d\mu(x_{m})\leq CR_{1}^{-3}F_{\mu}(R_{1})

for
C>0
depending only on
m
. Together with (
4.12
), this proves (
4.10
).
∎

With Lemma
4.2
, we are now ready to show Lemma
4.1
.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

We first show (
4.4
), proceeding by induction. The case
m=0
is trivial. We assume that (
4.4
) holds true for an exponent
m\in\mathbb{N}
and we estimate

\begin{split}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)\Psi\rangle&=\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\,\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m}(w)a_{x}^{*}a_{x}\Psi\rangle\\
&=\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\,\langle a_{x}\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m}a_{x}\Psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\rho^{-m/2-12m\varepsilon}+\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\,\langle a_{x}\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m}(w)a_{x}\Psi\rangle\,.\end{split}

(4.13)

With (
2.34
), applying Cauchy-Schwarz, recalling that
0\leq J(x)\leq 1
and noticing that
J(x)
and
\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)
commute (they are both multiplication operators, on each sector with fixed number of particles), we find

\begin{split}\langle&\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\rho^{-m/2-12m\varepsilon}+C(\rho R^{3})\rho^{-3\varepsilon/2}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\hskip 8.5359pt+(1+C\rho^{3\varepsilon/2})\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int dydy^{\prime}\nu(x-y)\nu(x-y^{\prime})\langle\Psi,J(y)a_{y}\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R}(w)a_{y^{\prime}}^{*}J(y^{\prime})\Psi\rangle\,,\end{split}

(4.14)

where we introduced the notation
\nu=\gamma^{-1}*\sigma
. Applying the induction assumption in the second term and commuting
a_{y},a_{y^{\prime}}^{*}
in the last term, we obtain

\begin{split}\langle&\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\rho^{-(m+1)/2-12(m+21/24)\varepsilon}\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+(1+C\rho^{3\varepsilon/2})\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dxdy\,\nu(x-y)^{2}\langle J(y)\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m}J(y)\Psi\rangle\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+(1+C\rho^{3\varepsilon/2})\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int dydy^{\prime}f_{\ell}(y-y^{\prime})^{2}\nu(x-y)\nu(x-y^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 22.76228pt\times\langle J(y^{\prime})a_{y^{\prime}}\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+\chi(|y-w|\leq R)+\chi(|y^{\prime}-w|\leq R))^{m}J(y)a_{y}\Psi\rangle\,.\end{split}

(4.15)

In the second term on the r.h.s. we use Lemma
2.2
to estimate
\|\nu\|_{2}^{2}\lesssim\rho^{3/2}
. In the third term, we use the fast decay of the kernels
\nu(z)
for
|z|\gg\ell_{0}\rho^{-\varepsilon/2}=C\rho^{-1/2-3\varepsilon/2}
, as established in Lemma
2.2
, to restrict the integrals to
|y-x|\leq\ell_{1}
,
|y^{\prime}-x|\leq\ell_{1}
, where
\ell_{1}=\ell_{0}\rho^{-3\varepsilon/4}=C\rho^{-1/2-7\varepsilon/4}
(with respect to the scale
\rho^{-1/2-3\varepsilon/2}
from (
2.27
) in Lemma
2.2
, we add an additional factor
\rho^{-\varepsilon/4}
to gain some smallness). Error terms can be bounded using the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. For example, to estimate the contribution

\begin{split}\text{A}:=&\,\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-x|\leq\ell_{1}\\
|y^{\prime}-x|\geq\ell_{1}\end{subarray}}dydy^{\prime}\,f_{\ell}^{2}(y-y^{\prime})\nu(x-y)\nu(x-y^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt\times\langle J(y^{\prime})a_{y^{\prime}}\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+\chi(|y-w|\leq R)+\chi(|y^{\prime}-w|\leq R))^{m}J(y)a_{y}\Psi\rangle\end{split}

(4.16)

we can bound the
y^{\prime}
-integral by a sum of integrals on balls of radius
\ell_{1}/2
, centered around lattice points
(\ell_{1}/2)n
, with
n=(n_{1},n_{2},n_{3})\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}\cap\Lambda
and
|n|=|n_{1}|+|n_{2}|+|n_{3}|\geq 2
. With (
2.27
) we obtain, for arbitrary
k\in\mathbb{N}
,

\begin{split}|\text{A}|\leq&\,C\rho^{1+\varepsilon k/4+\delta}\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:\ell_{1}n/2\in\Lambda,|n|\geq 2}\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int_{|y-x|\leq\ell_{1}}dydy^{\prime}\,|\nu(x-y)|\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\frac{\chi(|x-y^{\prime}-\ell_{1}n/2|\leq\ell_{1}/2)}{|n|^{k}}\|a_{y^{\prime}}(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m/2}\Psi\|\|a_{y}(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m/2}\Psi\|\end{split}

with a constant
C>0
depending on
k
. By Cauchy-Schwarz, we have

\begin{split}|\text{A}|&\leq C\rho^{-1/2+\varepsilon(k/4-12)+\delta}\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m}\mathcal{N}_{R+\ell_{1}}(w)\Psi\rangle^{1/2}\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt\times\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}:|n|\geq 2}|n|^{-k}\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+1)^{m}\mathcal{N}_{R+\ell_{1}/2}(w-\ell_{1}n/2)\Psi\rangle^{1/2}\,.\end{split}

From the translation invariance of
\Psi
and from Lemma
4.2
(since
R+\ell_{1}\leq 2R
), we find (fixing
k>3
to make sure that
\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}^{3}}|n|^{-k}<\infty
) a constant
C>0
depending on
k,m
such that

|\text{A}|\leq C\rho^{-1/2+\varepsilon(k/4-12)+\delta}\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}^{m+1}_{R}(w)+1)\Psi\rangle\,.

Choosing
k\in\mathbb{N}
sufficiently large, we conclude that, for any
\kappa>0
,

\begin{split}|\text{A}|&\leq\kappa\rho^{\varepsilon}\langle\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)+1)\Psi\rangle\end{split}

(4.17)

if
\rho>0
is small enough (depending on
m
). Hence, we obtain

\begin{split}\langle&\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\rho^{-(m+1)/2-12(m+21/24)\varepsilon}+\kappa\rho^{\varepsilon}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}_{R}^{m+1}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\hskip 11.38092pt+(1+C\rho^{3\varepsilon/2})\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int_{|y-x|\leq\ell_{1},|y^{\prime}-x|\leq\ell_{1}}dydy^{\prime}f_{\ell}(y-y^{\prime})^{2}\nu(x-y)\nu(x-y^{\prime})\\
&\hskip 56.9055pt\times\langle J(y^{\prime})a_{y^{\prime}}\Psi,(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+\chi(|y-w|\leq R)+\chi(|y^{\prime}-w|\leq R))^{m}J(y)a_{y}\Psi\rangle\,.\end{split}

(4.18)

In the last term, we expand
(\mathcal{N}_{R}(w)+\chi(|y-w|\leq R)+\chi(|y^{\prime}-w|\leq R))^{m}
; except for
\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R}(w)
, all other contributions can be bounded using the induction assumption (after applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality). For example,

\begin{split}&\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-x|\leq\ell_{1}\\
|y^{\prime}-x|\leq\ell_{1}\end{subarray}}dydy^{\prime}|\nu(x-y)||\nu(x-y^{\prime})|\|\mathcal{N}_{R}^{(m-1)/2}(w)a_{y^{\prime}}\Psi\|\|\mathcal{N}_{R}^{(m-1)/2}(w)a_{y}\Psi\|\\
&\leq\int_{|x-w|\leq R}dx\int_{\begin{subarray}{c}|y-x|\leq\ell_{1}\\
|y^{\prime}-x|\leq\ell_{1}\end{subarray}}dydy^{\prime}|\nu(x-y)|^{2}\|\mathcal{N}_{R}^{(m-1)/2}(w)a_{y^{\prime}}\Psi\|^{2}\\
&\leq C\rho^{-9\varepsilon}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}^{m-1}_{R}(w)\mathcal{N}_{R+\ell_{1}}(w)\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{-9\varepsilon}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R+\ell_{1}}(w)\Psi\rangle\\
&\leq C\rho^{-9\varepsilon}\langle\Psi,\mathcal{N}^{m}_{R}(w)\Psi\rangle\leq C\rho^{-m/2-12(m+3/4)\varepsilon}\,,\end{split}

where in the last line we applied Lemma
4.2
and the induction assumption.

We arrive at
